```
def fft_convolution(a, N):
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conj(F),N)
    c=c[:len(a)*2-1]
    # clip small negative
    if c has negative significant? For nonnegative, should nonneg within numerical; clip to 0.
    return c
```
For gradient with weights u length 2n-1:
```
def soft_obj_grad(x, n, N, beta):
    p=softmax(x)
    F=...
    c=irfft(F*conj(F),N)[:2n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0)
    # objective: const_factor * logsumexp(beta*(c - c_ref)) / beta
    # choose c_ref = max or mean? For numerical, cmax.
    cmax=np.max(c)
    z=beta*(c-cmax)
    # logsumexp stable: cmax*beta + log(sum exp(z))
    ls = cmax*beta + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(z))) # since exp(cmax*beta)*sum exp(beta(c-cmax))
    obj = const*ls/beta
    # u = exp(z)/sum exp(z)
    u = np.exp(z); usum=u.sum(); u/=usum
    # grad p = 2*irfft(fft(u)*conj(F)) first n
    # multiply by const? const=2n
    gp = 2*np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(u,N)*np.conj(F),N)[:n]
    # gradient x = p*(gp - dot(p,gp))
    gx = p*(gp - np.dot(p,gp))
    gx *= const
```

What about cross mix? Let's design periodic cross step:
- Given current f (normalized p). Compute b via L-BFGS minimize cross objective `cross_obj(x,beta)` with fixed f. Start b=f or best b from previous. Use maybe 60-120 iterations. - Could also set f=b if self of b improves.
To compute cross objective for b, `cross_fun(x, q, Fq, n, N, beta)`:
```
b=softmax(x)
Fb=rfft(b,N)
h=irfft(Fq*Fb,N)[:2n-1]
h=max(h,0)
u=softmax(beta*h)
obj = logsumexp(beta*h)/beta (or h max smooth)
grad_b=irfft(rfft(u,N)*conj(Fq),N)[:n]
grad_x=b*(grad_b - dot(b,grad_b))
return obj, grad_x
```

Detailed functions:

def active_mirror_step(x, n,N, top_frac=0.1, delta=0.02, step_factor=0.1):

   if M <=0: return x,p,sc, False

```
import numpy as np
import time
import scipy.optimize as scipy_opt
import scipy.linalg? maybe not.
import math

def stable_softmax(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).ravel()
    x=np.where(np.isfinite(x),x,0.0)
    mx=np.max(x)
    e=np.exp(x-mx)
    s=e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
       p=np.zeros(x.size); p[x==mx]=1/(+(x==mx).sum())
       return p
    return e/s
```

Potential underflow: if x-mx less than -745, exp=0; okay. If all zeros except one, fine.

```
def log_weights(p):
   p=np.where(p>1e-300,p,1e-300)
   w=np.log(p)
   w-=w.max()
   return w
```

```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); finite; max0; s; if s>0 return a/s else ones
```

   if empty return
   a=np.maximum(a,0); s; p=a/s

   idx=np.where(p>min_mass)[0]
   if none return a copy? 
   l=idx[0]; r=idx[-1]
   # Also trim where mass outside tiny: while l>0 and p[l-1]*? <1e-8 sum? We can use cum from edges.

   trailing similar.
   # internal zeros not removed.

```

```
def self_objective_and_grad(x, alpha, n, N):
    p=stable_softmax(x)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conjugate(F), N)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    beta=alpha*n
    z=beta*c
    m=np.max(z)
    e=np.exp(z-m)
    se=e.sum()
    if not finite or se<=0: ...
    ls=(m + np.log(se)) / beta
    obj=2.0*n*ls
    u=e/se
    grad_p=2.0*np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(u,N)*np.conjugate(F), N)
    grad_p=grad_p[:n]
    grad_p=np.where(np.isfinite(grad_p),grad_p,0.0)
    avg=float(np.dot(p,grad_p))
    grad_x=p*(grad_p - avg)
    grad_x*=2.0*n
    grad_x=np.where(np.isfinite(grad_x),grad_x,0.0)
    return obj, grad_x
```
Do we need to multiply by 2n for L-BFGS? yes. Potential huge gradients? c ~1e-4, grad_p ~? ∑u p ~1/n maybe 2e-4; times 2n ~0.4. okay.

def cross_objective_and_grad(x, q, Fq, alpha, n, N):
   b=stable_softmax(x)
   Fb=rfft(b,N)
   h=irfft(Fq*Fb,N)[:2n-1]; h=max0
   beta=alpha*n; z=beta*h; ... u
   ls=...; obj=2n*ls
   grad_b=irfft(rfft(u,N)*conj(Fq),N)[:n]
   avg=dot(b,grad_b); grad_x=b*(grad_b-avg)*2n
   return obj, grad_x
```

```
def run_lbgfs(x0, alpha, n, N, time_limit, fun, args, maxiter_est=60):
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline... 
    res=scipy_opt.minimize(x0, fun, args=args, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': maxiter_est, 'ftol':1e-13, 'gtol':1e-12, 'maxls':10})
    return res.x, res.fun
```
But helper needs deadline? Pass time_limit absolute? Could define top-level `lbfgs_chunk(x0, fun, args, maxiter, deadline)`.
```
def lbfgs_chunk(x0, fun, args, maxiter, deadline):
   if time.monotonic() >= deadline: return x0,None
   try:
      res=scipy_opt.minimize(x0, fun, args=args, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-12,'maxls':20})
   except Exception:
      return None,None
   x=res.x if finite else x0
   f=res.fun if finite else None
   return x,f
```
No closures. `fun` is top-level function.

Pseudo:
```
def exact_mirror_adam(x, n, N, time_limit, deadline, rng):
    best_x=x.copy(); p=softmax(x); best_sc=fft_score(p,n,N)
    lr=0.05
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - time_limit:
       p=softmax(best_x) # or current
       F=rfft(p,N); c=irfft(...)
       M=max(c)
       if not finite break
       idx = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:top]
       u=zeros(len(c)); # weights
       vals=c[idx]
       thresh=M - delta*(M - np.mean(vals))? maybe threshold = M - 0.1*M
       # use weights = (vals - threshold) where positive else 1
       th = M - 0.1*M
       w=np.maximum(vals-th,1e-300)
       u[idx]=w/sum(w)
       gp=2*irfft(rfft(u,N)*conj(F),N)[:n]
       gx=p*(gp-dot(p,gp))
       # Adam step
       t+=1; beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; ...
       step=mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
       step=np.clip(step,-10,10)
       # normalize step: target relative p change small
       # scale = lr / max(abs(step))? 
       for factor in [1, 0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]:
           x_try = best_x - lr*factor*step
           x_try = np.where(finite, x_try, best_x)
           p_try=softmax(x_try)
           sc=fft_score(p_try,n,N)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best_x=x_try; best_p...; best_sc=sc; stall=0; break
       else:
           stall+=1
           lr *= 0.8
           if stall>20: perturb best_x with noise; lr=0.05
    return best_x
```
Maybe `lr` should be relative to gradient scale. Adam normalizes per coordinate, step magnitude ~1. Subtracting lr*step changes logits by ~lr. lr 0.05 good. For n large, maybe 0.1. Scale step clipping.

Let's think about objective gradient derivation more rigorously to avoid sign errors. We minimize obj=2n*(1/β)logΣ exp(β c_k). ∂obj/∂p_i =2n*Σ_k u_k ∂c_k/∂p_i =2n*2? Wait ∂c_k/∂p_i = p_{k-i} + p_{k-i}? For c_k=Σ_j p_j p_{k-j}. Derivative wrt p_i: terms where j=i: p_{k-i} (if k-i valid in first factor). Terms where k-j=i => j=k-i: p_{k-i} (if valid in second factor). If both terms same when 2i=k, still derivative 2 p_i? The two occurrences may be same index but derivative of p_i^2 =2p_i. So ∂c_k/∂p_i=2 sum over valid? More precisely if 0≤k-i<n, both factors? Let's check: c_k=Σ_{j=0}^{n-1} p_j p_{k-j}, with p out of range 0. Differentiate: term j=i gives p_{k-i}; term j=k-i gives p_i. If j=i and j=k-i both happen when 2i=k, two terms, total 2p_i. If only one valid, one. But formula 2 * p_{k-i} with p out of range? If k-i valid, first term exists; second exists if k-i? Wait if j=k-i valid, p_{?}. Let's set derivative: d/dp_r Σ_j p_j p_{k-j} = Σ_j [δ_{j,r} p_{k-j} + p_j δ_{r,k-j}]. First = p_{k-r} if k-r in [0,n-1]. Second = p_{k-r} if k-r in [0,n-1]. So both equal same condition; total 2 p_{k-r} if valid else 0. Good. So ∂Σu c = 2 Σ_k u_k p_{k-r}. Yes gp=2 IDFT formula. Good.

Frequency formula for gp: gp_r =2Σ_j p_j u_{r+j} (cyclic). We derived DFT gp/2 = F_u * conj(F_p). Let's verify with simple p=uniform? Hard. But should be. Alternatively we can compute gp via direct convolution for small in tests? No. Use formula. For cross h_k=Σ_j q_j b_{k-j}. ∂h_k/∂b_r = q_{k-r}. Sum =Σ_j q_j u_{r+j}. DFT = F_u * conj(F_q). Good.

Use `np.conjugate(F)`. For real p, F Hermitian. `irfft` returns real length N.

Potential boundary: We use cyclic DFT length N≥2n-1. The convolution p*p in cyclic length N has for k=2n-2..N-1 zero (if p* p wraps? Since N≥2n-1, no wrap for k up to 2n-2, but for k>N? The cyclic conv for k>2n-2 equals linear conv at k? There is no wrap of linear conv because linear max index 2n-2 < N. So zeros. Good. For gradient using u length 2n-1 zero padded to N, formula cyclic gives for r 0..n-1: 2Σ_k u_k p_{k-r mod N}. Since k-r can be negative mod N; for k-r negative, the corresponding term should be 0 (linear), but cyclic uses p_{N + k-r}, which might include wrap contributions from u_k at small k? Wait critical! We used λ zero padded length N. In cyclic formula, dL/db_r =2Σ_k u_k b_{k-r mod N}. For k-r negative, cyclic index = k-r+N. But linear derivative should be 0 if k-r<0. Since u_k is zero for k≥2n-1. For k<2n-1 and r up to n-1, k-r can be as low as -(n-1). Cyclic index = N + k-r ∈ (N-(n-1), N). But b (p) has length n padded zeros beyond n-1. Since N≥2n, N + k-r ≥ N-(n-1) ≥ n+1? N at least 2n, so N-(n-1)≥n+1, outside p support (indices n..N-1 are zeros). Thus cyclic p at those indices is zero. Good. If N=2n-1? Need N≥2n? For k=0,r=n-1, k-r=-(n-1), cyclic=N-n+1. If N=2n-1, index=n, p_n=0 (p length n indices 0..n-1, padded zeros), okay. If N=2n-2, index n-1? Could wrap incorrectly. So choose N≥2n, power of two >=2n. Good. For cross q*b similarly q padded zero; cyclic negative indices refer q at large index zero if N≥2n. Good.

Thus gradient formula works with N≥2n.

Score c: `irfft(F*conj(F),N)` length N includes c for k=0..2n-2, zeros else. For nonnegative p, c nonnegative. Clip.

Now, initial noise generation:
```
def random_start(n, rng, kind=0):
   if kind==0: uniform
   if kind==1: x=rng.normal(0,1,n); p=softmax
   if kind==2: lowpass noise:

Resampling parent to alt length:
```
def resample_seq(p, m):
    if len(p)==m: return p
    old=np.linspace(0,1,len(p))
    new=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    vals=np.interp(new, old, p)
    return normalize_nonneg(vals)
```

def self_c(p, N):
   n=p.size
   F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conjugate(F),N)
   c=c[:2*n-1]
   c=np.where(c>0,c,0)
   # If numerical sum off, maybe normalize to preserve? For score no; if sum <=0 fallback.
   return F,c

def fft_score(p,n,N):
   F,c=self_c(p,N)
   mx=c.max()
   if not finite: return INF
   return 2.0*n*mx
```

`lbfgs_chunk`: Need pass `deadline` and `maxiter`.
```
def lbfgs_chunk(x0, fun, args, maxiter, deadline):
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline: return x0.copy(), None
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        # simple fallback: one gradient step? But fun returns obj,grad. Could do.
        try:
           f,g=fun(x0,*args)
           if finite: x1=x0-0.01*g? not good.
        except: pass
        return x0.copy(), None
    try:
       opts={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-12, 'maxls':20}
       res=scipy_opt.minimize(x0, fun, args=args, method='L-BFGS-B', options=opts)
    except Exception:
       return x0.copy(), None
    x=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): x=x0.copy()
    f=float(res.fun) if np.isfinite(getattr(res,'fun',np.nan)) else None
    return x,f
```
No closures. If SciPy fun expects x args, okay.

But if no scipy, we need still work. We have exact_mirror_adam as fallback. Good.

`self_objective_and_grad` as above. Need avoid division by beta if beta=0. alpha>0.
```
def self_objective_and_grad(x, alpha, n, N):
    p=stable_softmax(x)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conjugate(F),N).astype(float)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    beta=float(alpha)*float(n)
    z=beta*c
    # if all z zero? c sum maybe finite, if max 0 return large?
    m=float(np.max(z)) if z.size else 0.0
    # stable exponent: use z-m, but if m very large? okay.
    e=np.exp(np.clip(z-m, -745.0, 709.0)) # no, if z-m >709? m max so z-m <=0. For positive? no due -m, max 0. Safe. If m negative? z nonnegative c>=0, so m>=0. Good.
    se=float(np.sum(e))
    if not finite or se<=0: return INF, zeros_grad
    ls=(m + math.log(se)) / beta
    obj=2.0*n*ls
    u=e/se
    gu=np.fft.rfft(u,N)
    gp=2.0*np.fft.irfft(gu*np.conjugate(F),N)
    gp=gp[:n].astype(float)
    gp=np.where(np.isfinite(gp),gp,0.0)
    avg=float(np.dot(p,gp))
    gx=p*(gp-avg)
    gx*=2.0*n
    gx=np.where(np.isfinite(gx),gx,0.0)
    return obj,gx
```
If beta huge and m also huge, m/beta = cmax. `math.log(se)` where se from exp(z-m), se≥1. If z all zero, e=1, se=N, m=0, ls=log(N)/β small, not actual max? If c all zero impossible if p sum1. If c has max 1e-8, z=βc maybe 10? ok.
Potential overflow in `np.clip(z-m,-745,0)` not needed because z-m≤0, but due tiny positive? use `np.minimum(z-m,0.0)`. `np.exp` of -1000 underflows to0; okay. If m = inf? c finite. If c max small and beta huge, m finite. Good.

def cross_update(p, x, b_x, n,N, deadline, remaining_fraction, rng):
    # returns p_new, x_new, b_x_new, improved
    Fq,_=self_c(p,N)
    # ensure b_x finite
    if b_x is None or len != n or not finite: b_x=log_weights(p)
    alpha_cross=200.0
    current_best=fft_score(p,n,N)
    improved=False
    # up to 3 lbfgs chunks while time > 20
    for it in range(3):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 30: break
       b_x, f = lbfgs_chunk(b_x, cross_objective_and_grad, (p,Fq,alpha_cross,n,N), 50, deadline)
       b=stable_softmax(b_x)
       # evaluate b self

       if sc_b < current_best:
           p=b.copy(); x=log_weights(p); current_best=sc_b; improved=True; Fq,_=self_c(p,N); b_x=x? or log b
       # mixes
       for t in (0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9):
           cand=(1-t)*p + t*b
           s=cand.sum(); if s>0: cand/=s
           sc=fft_score(cand,n,N)
           if sc < current_best - 1e-12:
               p=cand.copy(); x=log_weights(p); current_best=sc; improved=True; Fq,_=self_c(p,N)
       if not improved:
           # maybe increase alpha_cross? keep? if failed, break
           if it>0: break
    return p,x,b_x,improved

Now, main `optimize_start`:
```
def optimize_start(p_in, n, time_budget, deadline, rng):
    start=time.monotonic()
    end = start + max(0,time_budget)
    p=normalize_nonneg(p_in); n=p.size
    if n != requested? if p_in length requested. Use p.size.
    N=next_fast_len(2*n)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_x=log_weights(p); best_score=fft_score(best_p,n,N)
    x=best_x.copy()
    b_x=x.copy()
    alpha=20.0
    no_improve=0
    chunk_count=0
    cross_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < end - 60: # leave 60 for mirror? if budget small no
       if time.monotonic() >= end: break
       if no_improve < 3:
           # self L-BFGS
           args=(alpha,n,N)
           x,ff=lbfgs_chunk(x, self_objective_and_grad, args, 60, end)
           p=stable_softmax(x)
           sc=fft_score(p,n,N)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
               best_score=sc; best_p=p.copy(); best_x=x.copy(); no_improve=0
               # maybe increase alpha slowly
               alpha=min(2500, alpha*1.2)
           else:
               no_improve +=1
               # if no improve, reset x to best or perturbed
               if no_improve==1:
                   x=best_x.copy()
               elif no_improve==2:
                   x=best_x + rng.normal(0,0.1,n)
               else:
                   x=best_x + rng.normal(0,0.3,n)
       elif no_improve < 7:
           # cross update
           p_new, x_new, b_x_new, improved = cross_update(best_p, x, b_x, n,N, end, alpha_cross=max(200,min(2000,alpha)), max_chunks=2)
           b_x=b_x_new
           if improved:
               best_p=p_new; best_x=log_weights(p_new); best_score=fft_score(best_p,n,N); x=log_weights(best_p); no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve +=1
           chunk_count +=1
       else:
           # increase alpha and restart
           alpha=min(5000, alpha*2.5)
           no_improve=0
           x=best_x.copy()
           # add small noise
           x = x + rng.normal(0,0.2,n)
       # every few chunks exact? skip inside, but maybe update best if current improves.
```
Potential issue: If L-BFGS returns x that improves smooth objective but FFT score not; threshold -1e-10. Could miss small improvements. Use epsilon relative: `if sc < best_score - 1e-12` okay. If no improvement after a chunk, reset.

However, after a successful self L-BFGS chunk, we update alpha by 1.2; this continuously sharpens. But if improvement tiny, alpha may go high too fast. Could only increase on stall. Better:
- Keep alpha constant for several improvements; increase when no_improve starts. Let alpha schedule by `no_improve`. In L-BFGS block: if improved no_improve=0, maybe alpha unchanged or slight *1.05. If no_improve increases, alpha *=1.5. At cross, alpha maybe current.

def exact_mirror_adam(x, n, N, deadline, rng):
    x=...; best_x=x.copy(); best_p=stable_softmax(x); best_sc=fft_score(best_p,n,N)
    lr=0.05
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; stall=0; delta_frac=0.15
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
       p=stable_softmax(best_x)  # start from best each step? Or current? Since we only accept improvements, best_x is current.
       F,c=self_c(p,N)
       M=float(np.max(c))
       if not finite: break
       # choose top count
       top_count = max(1, min(c.size, int(round(0.02*c.size))))
       # sort c descending. For n=5000 c size 10000; sort each step O(N logN) maybe okay but 1000 steps -> heavy. Use argpartition for top.
       if top_count < c.size:
           idx = np.argpartition(c, -top_count)[-top_count:]
       else: idx=np.arange(c.size)
       vals=c[idx]
       # weights: vals > M - delta_frac*M; if none, use top one
       thresh = M - delta_frac*M if M>0 else 0
       w=np.where(vals>thresh, vals-thresh, 0.0)
       if w.sum() <=0:
           # use largest few uniform
           top2=max(1,top_count//10)
           w2=np.abs(vals)-np.min(vals) + 1e-300? Easier: w=np.ones_like(vals)
           # emphasize largest? use vals**10 normalized
           w=vals**10 if vals max >0 else ones
           w=np.where(w>0,w,1e-300)
       w_sum=w.sum(); if w_sum<=0: w=ones; w_sum=top_count
       u=np.zeros(c.size)
       u[idx]=w/w_sum
       gp=2*irfft(rfft(u,N)*conj(F),N)[:n]
       gx=p*(gp-dot(p,gp))
       # Adam
       t+=1; b1=0.9; b2=0.999
       m=b1*m+(1-b1)*gx; v=b2*v+(1-b2)*gx*gx
       mhat=m/(1-b1**t); vhat=v/(1-b2**t)
       step=mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
       step=np.where(finite,step,0)
       step=np.clip(step,-50,50)
       # scale: if max abs step large, factor=lr / max(1, maxabs)
       maxabs=max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(step))))
       scale=lr/maxabs
       accepted=False
       for factor in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125):
           x_try=best_x - scale*factor*step
           p_try=stable_softmax(x_try)
           sc=fft_score(p_try,n,N)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best_x=x_try; best_p=p_try; best_sc=sc; stall=0; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
          stall +=1
          if stall % 5 ==0:
             lr = max(0.002, lr*0.7)
             delta_frac = min(0.5, delta_frac + 0.05) # include more peaks
             # occasionally perturb
             if stall > 20:
                sigma=0.05+0.05*(stall/20)
                x_try=best_x + sigma*rng.normal(0,1,n)
                p_try=stable_softmax(x_try); sc=fft_score(...)
                if sc < best_sc: accept? no, do for extra.
                best_x=x_try? maybe even if worse? Not monotonic. Could keep best and set current? Since we always use best_x, perturbation would discard best. Instead we can maintain a `current_x` separate for Adam, but exact mirror from best each step smooths. Let's implement with current_x.
```

`trim_edges` implementation carefully:
```
def trim_edges(p, edge_mass=1e-7):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p)
    n=len(p)
    if n==0: return p
    cum=np.cumsum(p)
    left=0
    # while left<n and cum[left] <= edge_mass: left +=1
    # But if p[0]=0.5, cum[0]=0.5 > edge, no trim.
    # If first several tiny total=edge, left moves to first index where cumulative > edge.
    while left < n and cum[left] <= edge_mass:
        left += 1
    rev=p[::-1]
    rev_cum=np.cumsum(rev)
    right=n-1
    while right >= 0 and rev_cum[n-1-right] <= edge_mass:
        right -=1
    if right <= left:
        # if almost all mass tiny? return p maybe or one with max
        return p
    return p[left:right+1]
```
If leading zeros p0=0, cum[0]=0 <= edge, left increments. Good. If total mass <=edge? Then left may reach n; return p? But sum normalized so total1, edge small, left <n.

Zeroing internal below threshold: if p length 4806, p<thresh maybe none. For random no.

Could also shrink length by removing a prefix/suffix whose mass is less than 1e-5 not just 1e-7. To improve, more trimming may reduce n maybe objective lower or higher. We can test edge masses [1e-7,1e-5,1e-3] but each exact expensive. In final, try a few and choose exact best. If trimming too much can worsen; exact decides. Use 3 edge masses maybe [1e-7,1e-5]. Good.

def random_lowpass_start(n, rng, frac=0.5):
    x=np.zeros(n)
    F=np.fft.rfft(x) # zero? better generate noise:
    noise = rng.normal(0,1,n)

    freq=np.arange(np.size(F))
    cutoff=int(frac*n)
    F[freq>cutoff]=0
    x=np.fft.irfft(F,n)

Let's examine possible bug in self_objective_and_grad objective derivative with logsumexp: We compute `ls = (m + log(se))/beta` where m=beta*cmax. This equals βcmax/β + log(se)/β = cmax + log(se)/β. But true logsumexp(βc)/β = cmax + (log(se))/β if se = sum exp(β(c-cmax)). Correct. Gradient wrt c = e^{β(c-cmax)}/se = u. Good.

For high α, `se` may be near 1 if one lag dominates; log(se) small. Objective close cmax.

The gradient in x: `gx=p*(gp-avg)*2n`. But wait `gp` already includes 2 for self, and ∂(Σu c)=gp. ∂obj/∂p=2n*gp. For softmax, ∂/∂x_i = dp_i/dx_j ∂obj/∂p_i? We used `gx = p*(gp - dot(p,gp))` times 2n. This formula for H(p) differentiable wrt p: ∂H/∂x_i = p_i(∂H/∂p_i - ∑ p_j ∂H/∂p_j). Yes.

Cross objective: H=2n*ls, ∂H/∂b_i=2n*grad_b (no 2). Formula same.

Now, let's think if there is a theoretical better construction to use directly. Could search using "gradient flow in Fourier" maybe. But code time. We can perhaps embed a construction based on solving a PDE? Maybe not.

But if parent was already optimized by similar methods, maybe stuck. Need different idea to guarantee meaningful improvement. Let's brainstorm alternative algorithmic ideas more.

1. **Exploit symmetry / reversed sequence**: The objective for p is invariant under p -> reversed p. Also under cyclic shift? If we shift sequence by s within length? Shifting changes support index: if p length n and shift circular, convolution wraps and length effectively? If add zeros to make same length and shift, support may wrap, not equivalent. But if we cyclically shift a full-length sequence with no boundary? Original conv of shifted linear sequence may wrap in frequency, not same; because support endpoints move. However if we consider circular convolution length n, shifting invariant. Not directly. Reversal same score. Could enforce symmetry? If p is symmetric p_i=p_{n-1-i}, then convolution c has center? Does symmetry help flatten? It makes c symmetric (already). It may reduce degrees but maybe not optimal. Don't enforce.

2. **Use iterative reweighting of lags with *dual* λ solved as maximum entropy**: Instead of λ=softmax(c), choose λ to make ∇c constant (KKT). We can solve λ with nonnegative least squares using FFT accelerator. This could yield better direction than high softmax. Let's explore implementable:

Now, code details for manual top selection without lambda:
```
def top_candidates_by_score(cands, k=4, max_n=20000):
    scored=[]
    for p in cands:
        p=normalize_nonneg(p)
        if p.size <= max_n:
            N=next_fast_len(2*p.size)
            sc=fft_score(p,p.size,N)
        else:
            sc=INF
        scored.append((sc,p))
    chosen=[]
    remaining=scored[:]
    while remaining and len(chosen)<k:
        bi=0; bs=INF
        for i, item in enumerate(remaining):
            if item[0] < bs:
                bs=item[0]; bi=i
        chosen.append(remaining.pop(bi))
    return [p for _,p in chosen]
```

   t=trim_edges(p,edge)
   if t.size>0: variants.append(t)
# zero
for thresh in (1e-10,1e-8):
   z=p.copy(); mask=z<thresh
   if np.any(mask):
      z[mask]=0; s=z.sum(); if s>0:
         z=z/s; variants.append(z)
         t=trim_edges(z,1e-7); if t.size>0: variants.append(t)
# Limit variants to 4 if n large
if n > 8000 and len(variants)>3: variants=variants[:3]
elif len(variants)>5: variants=variants[:5]
best=None; bestsc=INF
for v in variants:
    sc=exact_score(v)
    if sc<bestsc: bestsc=sc; best=v
if best is None: best=p
return normalize_nonneg(best), bestsc
```

Now, `finalize_sequence`:
```
def finalize_sequence(p):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p)
    n=p.size
    if n==0: p=np.ones(64); n=64
    target=np.sqrt(2.0*float(n))
    s=float(p.sum())
    if s>0: p=p*target/s
    np.clip(p,0,1000,out=p)
    s2=float(p.sum())
    if s2<0.01:
       p=p*(0.01/s2)
       np.clip(p,0,1000,out=p)
    # if max >1000 due clipping, sum may be less; okay.
    return [float(x) for x in p]
```

Now, code for `exact_mirror_adam` with `delta_frac` dynamic. Need `rng.normal` generation each stall; okay.

Let's write it carefully:
```
def exact_mirror_adam(x, n, N, deadline, rng):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).copy()
    if x.size != n: x=log_weights(np.ones(n))
    best_x=x.copy()
    best_p=stable_softmax(x)
    best_sc=fft_score(best_p,n,N)
    x_cur=best_x.copy()
    lr=0.05
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; stall=0; delta=0.2
    top_frac=0.02
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
        p=stable_softmax(x_cur)
        F,c=self_c(p,N)
        M=float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M): break
        total=c.size
        top_count=max(1, int(total*top_frac))
        if top_count < total:
            idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_count)[-top_count:]
        else:
            idx=np.arange(total)
        vals=c[idx]
        if M > 0:
            ratio=vals/M
            w=np.maximum(ratio - (1.0 - delta), 0.0)
            ws=w.sum()
            if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0:
                w=ratio*ratio + 1e-8
                ws=w.sum()
        else:
            w=np.ones_like(vals); ws=float(vals.size)
        if ws <=0 or not finite: w=np.ones_like(vals); ws=float(vals.size)
        u=np.zeros(total); u[idx]=w/ws
        gp=2.0*np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(u,N)*np.conjugate(F), N)[:n]
        gp=np.where(np.isfinite(gp),gp,0.0)
        gx=p*(gp - float(np.dot(p,gp)))
        gx=np.where(np.isfinite(gx),gx,0.0)
        t+=1; b1=0.9; b2=0.999
        m=b1*m+(1-b1)*gx
        v=b2*v+(1-b2)*gx*gx
        mhat=m/(1.0 - b1**t)
        vhat=v/(1.0 - b2**t)
        step=mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
        step=np.where(np.isfinite(step),step,0.0)
        step=np.clip(step,-100.0,100.0)
        maxabs=float(np.max(np.abs(step))) if step.size else 1.0
        if maxabs <=0: maxabs=1.0
        scale=lr/maxabs
        accepted=False
        # Try a few factors. To save time, maybe 3.
        for factor in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
            x_try=x_cur - scale*factor*step
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(x_try)):
                x_try=x_cur
            p_try=stable_softmax(x_try)
            sc=fft_score(p_try,n,N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                x_cur=x_try; best_x=x_try; best_p=p_try; best_sc=sc; stall=0; accepted=True; break
        if not accepted:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 5:
                lr=max(0.005, lr*0.75)
                delta=min(0.8, delta+0.05)
            elif stall == 15:
                x_cur=best_x + 0.1*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
                m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0; lr=0.04
                stall=0
            elif stall == 40:
                x_cur=best_x + 0.3*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
                m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0; lr=0.06
                stall=0
                delta=0.4
            elif stall > 60:
                x_cur=best_x.copy()
                stall=0
                delta=0.3
    # Maybe if x_cur better but not accepted? best_sc tracks.
    return best_x, best_p, best_sc
```
Potential: If x_cur perturbed worse, the next step gradient from worse could be large and maybe accept a jump back. Good. But if it improves from x_cur to a score between current x_cur and best, but not below best, we don't accept; best unchanged, x_cur may be worse. We may keep x_cur worse forever. At stall resets set to best/noise. Fine.

Should we update best if `sc < best_sc` but also if x_cur has lower than best due perturbation? Perturbation random won't lower. L-BFGS current may lower but not accepted? We only compute candidates from mirror step; if none accepted, x_cur unchanged (unless after accepted). If after stall perturbation, x_cur worse; okay.

Now, `self_c` function returns F,c. Need ensure p normalized? We pass normalized. For score, if p not sum1? In cross_update, b from softmax sum1; mix normalized. Good.

Now, `optimize_start` implementation in detail:
```
def optimize_start(p_in, time_budget, rng):
    start=time.monotonic()
    if time_budget <= 5: return p_in, fft_score(p_in,...)
    p=normalize_nonneg(p_in)
    n=p.size
    if n < 16: p=np.ones(4096)/4096; n=4096
    if n > MAX_N: p=resample_seq(p, MAX_N); n=p.size
    N=next_fast_len(2*n)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_x=log_weights(p); best_score=fft_score(best_p,n,N)
    x=best_x.copy()
    b_x=best_x.copy()
    time_budget=float(time_budget)
    reserve = max(20.0, 0.20*time_budget) if HAVE_SCIPY else 0.0
    phase_end = start + max(time_budget - reserve, 0.0)
    hard_end = start + time_budget
    phases=...
    cross_count=0
    cross_limit=3 if n>8000 else 5
    for phase_i, (alpha, ent) in enumerate(phases):
        if time.monotonic() >= phase_end: break
        inner=0
        inner_no=0
        while time.monotonic() < phase_end - 10:
            # run one L-BFGS chunk
            if not HAVE_SCIPY: break
            args=(float(alpha), float(ent), int(n), int(N))
            x_new, f = lbfgs_chunk(x, self_objective_and_grad, args, 50, phase_end)
            if x_new is not None:
                x=x_new
            p_cur=stable_softmax(x)
            sc=fft_score(p_cur,n,N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                best_score=sc; best_p=p_cur.copy(); best_x=x.copy()
                x=best_x.copy()  # continue from improved
                inner_no=0
                # print progress maybe
                print(f"[{tag}] phase alpha={alpha} ent={ent} score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)
            else:
                inner_no += 1
                if inner_no >= 3:
                    break
                # perturb
                sig = 0.02 * (inner_no)
                x = best_x + sig*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
            inner +=1
            if inner >= 2 and time.monotonic() >= phase_end - 20: break
        # after phase, maybe cross
        if time.monotonic() < phase_end and cross_count < cross_limit and (phase_i % 2 == 0 or best_score > 1.55):
            x, b_x, improved = cross_update_inplace(best_p, b_x, n, N, phase_end, rng)
            cross_count +=1
            if improved:
                # best_p updated? cross_update returns p? Let's design.
```
Need design cross_update return new best p/x/b_x. Let's make top-level `cross_update(p, b_x, n, N, deadline, rng)`:
- Assumes p is current best. Optimizes b, mixes, returns `(p_best, x_best, b_x_new, improved, best_score)`. It can update p if improved.
```
def cross_update(p, b_x, n,N,deadline,rng):
    q=p.copy(); N...; Fq,_=self_c(q,N); current=fft_score(q,n,N); best_p=q.copy(); best_x=log_weights(q); best_sc=current; improved=False
    if b_x is None or len mismatch/not finite: b_x=best_x.copy()
    alpha_cross=400.0
    for chunk in range(2):
       if now >= deadline-20: break
       b_x, _ = lbfgs_chunk(b_x, cross_objective_and_grad, (q,Fq,alpha_cross,n,N), 50, deadline)
       b=stable_softmax(b_x)
       # evaluate b
       scb=fft_score(b,n,N)
       if scb < best_sc - 1e-10:
           best_p=b.copy(); best_sc=scb; best_x=log_weights(best_p); improved=True
       for t in ...:
           cand=(1-t)*q + t*b; normalize; sc=...
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-10: update
    return best_p, best_x, b_x, improved, best_sc
```

if improved:

Now, `resample_seq` for p to new length:
```
def resample_seq(p, m):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p); m=int(max(16,m))
    if p.size == m: return p.copy()
    if p.size == 1:
       return np.full(m,1.0/m)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.1,0.999,p.size) ? If endpoints zero? Use 0,1.
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    vals=np.interp(x_new, np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size), p)
    return normalize_nonneg(vals)
```
If p has strong endpoints, interp extends. okay.

`random_start(n, rng)`:
```
def random_start(n, rng):
   n=int(max(16,n))
   # combine lowpass random with sinusoids
   x=np.zeros(n)
   freqs=[1,2,3,4,5] random? maybe random lowpass all modes below n/4.
   noise=rng.normal(0,1,n)
   F=np.fft.rfft(noise)
   cutoff=max(1, n//4)
   F[cutoff:]=0
   x=np.fft.irfft(F,n)
   # add smooth random
   for k in range(1,4):
       x += rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)*np.sin(2*np.pi*k*np.arange(n)/n + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   p=stable_softmax(x)
   # ensure not uniform? add tiny noise
   p=normalize_nonneg(p)
   return p
```
No lambda. Use `np.arange`; okay.

Final flow code:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    try: budget=float(budget_s)
    except: budget=2400.0
    budget=max(60.0, min(float(budget), 2395.0))
    hard_end = t0 + budget - 5.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    starts=collect_starts()
    if not starts: starts=[np.ones(4096,dtype=float)/4096]
    starts=sort_starts_by_score(starts,3)
    p0=prepare_length(starts[0]) # cap
    results=[(p0, fft_score(p0,p0.size,next_fast_len(2*p0.size)))]
    # main
    main_time=budget*0.55
    if main_time > 60:
       p_res, sc_res = optimize_start(p0, main_time, rng, tag='main')
       results.append((p_res, sc_res))
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=hard_end-now
    # alt up
    if rem > 300:
       m_up=choose_alternating_length(p0.size, 1.25? maybe 1.5, upper=16384)
       if m_up != p0.size:
          p_up=resample_seq(p0,m_up)
          time_up=min(rem*0.4, 800)
          if time_up>60:
             p_res, sc=optimize_start(p_up, time_up, rng, tag='up')
             results.append((p_res,sc))
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=hard_end-now
    # random same/down
    if rem > 200:
       m_rand = p0.size if p0.size <= 8192 else 8192
       p_rand=random_start(m_rand,rng)
       # maybe combine with p0: 0.5 p0 resampled + 0.5 random? Could help.
       p_rand = normalize_nonneg(0.5*resample_seq(p0,m_rand)+0.5*p_rand)
       time_rand=min(rem*0.5, 400)
       if time_rand>40:
          p_res,sc=optimize_start(p_rand,time_rand,rng,tag='rand')
          results.append((p_res,sc))
    # final cleanup with remaining but no new search
    now=time.monotonic(); rem=hard_end-now
    # include p0 and results; select top by FFT
    unique candidates? maybe list.
    top = top_candidates_by_score([p for p,_ in results], k=4)
    # incorporate p0 separately maybe top includes.
    best_final=None; best_exact=INF
    time_left=max(5, rem)
    max_variants = 1 if time_left<60 else 3 if time_left<180 else 5
    for p in top:
       if time.monotonic() >= hard_end - 3: break
       # cleanup one at a time, but cleanup_best uses exact maybe multiple; we need limit. Implement `cleanup_best_limited(p, time_left, max_variants)`.
```
Need a function `cleanup_best_limited(p, time_limit, max_variants)` top-level. It will generate variants up to max, exact evaluate until time.
```
def cleanup_best_limited(p, time_limit, max_variants):
    start=time.monotonic()
    p=normalize_nonneg(p)
    variants=[p]
    if max_variants>1:
       t=trim_edges(p,1e-7); if t.size: variants.append(t)
    if max_variants>2:
       t2=trim_edges(p,1e-5); if t2.size: variants.append(t2)
    if max_variants>3:
       z=p.copy(); threshold=1e-10; mask=z<threshold; if any: ... append z
    if max_variants>4:
       trim z append
    # Limit to time: estimate exact time by n? just loop check.
    best=None; bestsc=INF
    for v in variants:
        if time.monotonic() - start > max(0.5, time_limit*0.8): break
        if v.size > MAX_EXACT_N? If n huge >20000, skip exact? But cap.
        sc=exact_score(v)
        if finite and sc<bestsc: bestsc=sc; best=v
    if best is None:
        # use FFT best and no exact? Still return
        best=p; bestsc=fft_score(p,...)
    return best,bestsc
```
But we need choose overall across candidates. If we stop early for one, maybe other not exact. Use total deadline:
```
best_final=None; best_exact=INF; final_start=time.monotonic(); final_budget = max(10, hard_end - final_start)
for p in top:
    remaining = hard_end - time.monotonic()

def collect_starts():
    found=[]
    for name in list(globals().keys()):
        if name.startswith('height_sequence'):
            val = globals().get(name)
            try:
                arr = np.asarray(val, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            except Exception:
                continue
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            s = float(arr.sum())
            if arr.size >= 16 and s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
                p = arr / s
                found.append(p)
    if not found:
        found.append(np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64) / 4096.0)
    return found
```

def sort_starts_by_score(starts, limit=3):
    arr=[]
    for p in starts:
       p=normalize_nonneg(p)
       if p.size > MAX_N_SEARCH: p=resample_seq(p, 8192)
       arr.append(p)
    chosen=[]
    rem=arr[:]
    while rem and len(chosen)<limit:
       best_i=0; best_sc=INF
       for i,p in enumerate(rem):
           if p.size <= MAX_N_SEARCH:
               N=next_fast_len(2*p.size)
               sc=fft_score(p,p.size,N)
           else: sc=INF
           if sc < best_sc:
               best_sc=sc; best_i=i
       chosen.append(rem.pop(best_i))
    return chosen
```

def choose_alt_length(n, factor, upper, lower):
    m=int(round(n*factor))
    # round to 128
    step=128
    if m > 0: m=int(round(m/step)*step)
    m=max(lower,m); m=min(upper,m)
    return m

def cross_update(p, b_x, n, N, deadline, rng):
    q=normalize_nonneg(p)
    Fq, _ = self_c(q,N)
    best_p=q.copy(); best_score=fft_score(q,n,N); best_x=log_weights(q); improved=False
    if b_x is None or len(b_x)!=n or not np.all(np.isfinite(b_x)):
       b_x=log_weights(q)
    alpha_cross=400.0
    for chunk in range(2):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 20 or not HAVE_SCIPY: break
       maxiter = 25 if n>12000 else 40
       args=(q,Fq,alpha_cross,n,N)
       bx, _ = lbfgs_chunk(b_x, cross_objective_and_grad, args, maxiter, deadline)
       if bx is not None and finite: b_x=bx
       b=stable_softmax(b_x)
       scb=fft_score(b,n,N)
       if np.isfinite(scb) and scb < best_score - 1e-10:
          best_score=scb; best_p=b.copy(); best_x=log_weights(best_p); improved=True
       for tval in (0.25,0.4,0.55,0.7,0.85):
          cand=(1-tval)*q + tval*b
          s=float(np.sum(cand))
          if not finite or s<=0: continue
          cand=cand/s
          sc=fft_score(cand,n,N)
          if finite and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
             best_score=sc; best_p=cand.copy(); best_x=log_weights(best_p); improved=True
    return best_p,best_x,b_x,improved,best_score
```

Let's implement `self_c`:
```
def self_c(p, N):
    n=p.size
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conjugate(F), N)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    c=np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c=np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return F, c
```
For cross `cross_c(q,b,N,Fq=None)`: if Fq None compute.
```
def cross_b(q,b,N,Fq=None):
    n=q.size
    if Fq is None: Fq=np.fft.rfft(q,N)
    Fb=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(Fq*Fb,N)[:2*n-1]
    h=np.where(np.isfinite(h),h,0.0); h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
    return Fq,h
```

Our time domain derivative for convolution c = p*p gave D=∑ p_j u_{r+j}, DFT=conj(F_p) F_u. For autocorrelation r with shift r-j, DFT=F_p F_u.

So to compute p*p coefficients, use `irfft(F * F, N)`. For gradient weighted by u, use `2*irfft(np.fft.rfft(u,N) * np.conjugate(F), N)`. I had that for gradient, but c computation must be `F*F`, not `F*conj(F)`. Cross h=q*b: coefficients = irfft(Fq * Fb). Gradient wrt b: h_k=∑ q_j b_{k-j}; d/db_r=∑ q_j u_{r+j}; DFT = conj(F_q) F_u. Same formula with Fq. Good. So only c/h computation needs F*F and Fq*Fb.

This is a major fix. What about objective c nonnegative? p*p coefficients nonnegative. `F*F` IDFT should be real? Since F^2 Hermitian as shown, real. Good. Some tiny imaginary discarded by irfft? irfft assumes Hermitian input; if F^2 is not exactly Hermitian due rounding, might produce issues. We can compute full `np.fft.ifft(F*F)` and take real? For real coefficient, use `np.fft.ifft(F*F, N).real`. But r*? `irfft` expects Hermitian; F*F for real p should satisfy within rounding. To be safe, use `np.fft.ifft(F*F, N).real`? That uses complex full FFT and ifft N; more expensive but okay. `irfft` of F*F may be correct if F is rfft (Hermitian) and product F*F may not be strictly Hermitian due numerical? It should: rfft contains half; irfft reconstructs assuming imaginary part corresponds. If product not exactly, irfft may give wrong? Numpy irfft treats input as real FFT of a real sequence; if input isn't Hermitian-consistent, it ignores? It uses only half to reconstruct, effectively assumes Hermitian. F*F of a Hermitian F is Hermitian mathematically; rounding may break but small. Use `np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)`. Good.

Similarly cross h=irfft(Fq*Fb) where Fq,Fb from real q,b; product? For convolution q*b, DFT C=Fq*Fb. Is C Hermitian for real q,b? C(-m)=Fq(-m)Fb(-m)=conj(Fq)conj(Fb)=conj(C). Yes. Good.

Thus modify `self_c`: `c=np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)`. But wait for p real, F(m)^2 may have real DFT; yes. Let's test small p=[1,0,0,1], n=4, N=8. Conv linear [1,0,0,2,0,0,1]. F^2 IDFT should yield. F*conj(F) would yield autocorr [1,0,1,0,1,0,0,0]? wrong. So fix.

Now, gradient formula with conj(F) for convolution? Let's verify with p delta at 1 and u delta at 2, as before. c(p*p) for p δ1 is δ2. L=u δ2 ->L=1. Gradient dL/dp at p? dL/dp_r = 2? But p is fixed δ, derivative of p*p at δ? Let's test with p=[0,a,0] where variable a? If p has p_1=a, c_2=a^2. At a=1, L=1, derivative=2. DFT p: F=e^{-i2πm/8}; u δ2: U=e^{-i2πm4/8}=e^{-iπm}. Gradient formula 2 IDFT(U*conj(F)) =2 IDFT(e^{-iπm} e^{+i2πm/8})=2 IDFT(e^{-i2πm(4-1)/8})=2 δ1. correct. Great.

Now, objective logsumexp over c from F*F. Good.

Previous SOTA code used `A * A` for c? Yes, they had `A * A` in fft_score_A_c. They used `phase*conj(A)` in gradient due boundary? Our formula simpler.

Need ensure `F * F` for large values not overflow. p sum1, F max1, square max1. good.

Now, if p has negative? no.

Correct functions:
```

```
For cross: h=irfft(Fq*Fb,N).

W from c, c real not necessarily Hermitian in length N? W length 2n-1 zero padded. Is W^{(-m)}? Not necessarily because W depends on c (symmetric around n-1, not cyclic). But the product with conj(F) and IDFT corresponds to time derivative; result real? Mathematically gradient real. Numpy irfft again assumes Hermitian; is `W*conj(F)` Hermitian? W is real arbitrary not necessarily cyclic symmetric. Let's check: DFT of real gradient should be Hermitian. G(m)=2 U(m) conj(F(m)). We need G(-m)=conj(G(m))=2 conj(U(m)) F(m). But U(-m)=conj(U(m)) if u real? u is real sequence zero-padded, so U(-m)=conj(U(m)). Then G(-m)=2 U(-m) conj(F(-m))=2 conj(U(m)) F(m) (since conj(F(-m))=F(m)). Yes Hermitian. Good. So irfft safe.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing

    2 * n * max(np.convolve(a, a)[a]) / (sum(a)**2)

by working in the scale-invariant normalization sum(a)=1.

The method uses several complementary components:
  - smoothed L-infinity / entropy objectives in log-probability space,
  - L-BFGS-B polishing with analytically exact gradients using FFT,
  - a convex cross-correlation subproblem (min max(f * b)) for mixing,
  - an exact maximum-peak Adam mirror-descent polish,
  - trimming / zeroing clean-up with exact np.convolve validation.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.optimize as scipy_opt
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

INF = float("inf")
MAX_SEARCH_N = 12000
MAX_EXACT_N = 12000


def next_fast_len(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    p = 1
    while p < int(m):
        p <<= 1
    return p


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    """Return a finite non-negative vector normalized to sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        arr = np.ones(max(1, arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.size)
    return arr / s


def log_weights(p, floor=1e-18):
    """Log-weights of a non-negative probability vector, centered."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    p = np.maximum(p, float(floor))
    p = p / float(p.sum())
    w = np.log(p)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
    w -= float(np.max(w))
    return w


def stable_softmax(x):
    """Stable softmax returning a probability vector."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    mx = float(np.max(x))
    e = np.exp(x - mx)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros(x.size, dtype=np.float64)
        idx = np.where(x == mx)[0]
        p[idx] = 1.0 / float(idx.size)
        return p
    return e / s


def resample_seq(p, m):
    """Linear resampling of a sequence to a new length."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    m = int(max(128, m))
    if p.size == m:
        return p.copy()
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    vals = np.interp(new, old, p)
    return normalize_nonneg(vals)


def random_start(n, rng):
    """A random band-limited non-negative start."""
    n = int(max(128, n))
    x = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    noise = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
    F = np.fft.rfft(noise)
    cutoff = max(1, n // 4)
    F[cutoff:] = 0.0
    x += np.fft.irfft(F, n)

    for k in range(1, 4):
        x += rng.uniform(0.2, 1.0) * np.sin(
            2.0 * np.pi * float(k) * np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
            + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        )

    p = stable_softmax(x)
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    return p


def self_c(p, N):
    """FFT linear self-convolution p*p and its DFT F."""
    n = int(p.size)
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)
    c = c[: 2 * n - 1]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return F, c


def fft_score(p, n, N):
    """Fast FFT score for a normalized non-negative sequence."""
    _, c = self_c(p, N)
    if c.size == 0:
        return INF
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return INF
    return 2.0 * float(n) * mx


def exact_score(p):
    """Exact O(n^2) score using numpy.convolve."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return INF
    b = np.convolve(p, p)
    if b.size == 0:
        return INF
    mx = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return INF
    return 2.0 * float(n) * mx


def prepare_start(p):
    """Normalize and cap starting sequence length."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    if p.size < 128:
        p = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64) / 4096.0
    if p.size > MAX_SEARCH_N:
        p = resample_seq(p, min(8192, MAX_SEARCH_N))
    return p


def collect_starts():
    """Collect previously found sequences from globals if available."""
    found = []
    for name in list(globals().keys()):
        if name.startswith("height_sequence"):
            val = globals().get(name, None)
            try:
                arr = np.asarray(val, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            except Exception:
                continue
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            s = float(arr.sum())
            if arr.size >= 16 and np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
                found.append(arr / s)
    if not found:
        found.append(np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64) / 4096.0)
    return found


def sort_starts_by_score(starts, limit=3):
    """Keep a few of the best initial sequences by FFT score."""
    arr = []
    for p in starts:
        p = prepare_start(p)
        arr.append(p)

    chosen = []
    remaining = arr[:]
    while remaining and len(chosen) < limit:
        bi = 0
        bs = INF
        for i, p in enumerate(remaining):
            if p.size <= MAX_EXACT_N:
                N = next_fast_len(2 * p.size)
                sc = fft_score(p, p.size, N)
            else:
                sc = INF
            if sc < bs:
                bs = sc
                bi = i
        chosen.append(remaining.pop(bi))
    return chosen


def top_candidates_by_score(cands, k=4):
    """Select top candidates by FFT score without lambdas."""
    scored = []
    for p in cands:
        p = normalize_nonneg(p)
        if p.size <= MAX_EXACT_N:
            N = next_fast_len(2 * p.size)
            sc = fft_score(p, p.size, N)
        else:
            sc = INF
        scored.append((sc, p))

    chosen = []
    remaining = scored[:]
    while remaining and len(chosen) < k:
        bi = 0
        bs = INF
        for i, item in enumerate(remaining):
            if item[0] < bs:
                bs = item[0]
                bi = i
        chosen.append(remaining.pop(bi))
    return [p for _, p in chosen]


def lbfgs_chunk(x0, fun, args, maxiter, deadline):
    """Run a bounded L-BFGS-B chunk."""
    x0 = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
        return x0, None
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return x0, None

    maxiter = int(max(1, maxiter))
    n = int(x0.size)
    if n > 12000:
        maxiter = min(maxiter, 25)
    elif n > 6000:
        maxiter = min(maxiter, 35)

    try:
        opts = {
            "maxiter": maxiter,
            "ftol": 1e-13,
            "gtol": 1e-9,
            "maxls": 20,
        }
        res = scipy_opt.minimize(
            x0, fun, args=args, method="L-BFGS-B", options=opts
        )
    except Exception:
        return x0, None

    x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != x0.size or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        x = x0.copy()

    f = None
    rf = getattr(res, "fun", None)
    if rf is not None:
        try:
            rf = float(rf)
            if np.isfinite(rf):
                f = rf
        except Exception:
            f = None
    return x, f


def self_objective_and_grad(x, alpha, ent, n, N):
    """
    Smooth objective:

        2n * logsumexp(beta*c)/beta  +  ent*(Hmax - H(c)),

    where c = p*p, p = softmax(x).
    """
    n = int(n)
    p = stable_softmax(x)
    F, c = self_c(p, N)

    beta = float(alpha) * float(n)
    if beta <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(beta):
        beta = 1.0

    z = beta * c
    m = float(np.max(z)) if z.size else 0.0
    ez = np.exp(np.maximum(z - m, -745.0))
    se = float(ez.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(se) or se <= 0.0:
        obj, grad = INF, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        return obj, grad

    u = ez / se
    ls = (m + math.log(se)) / beta
    obj = 2.0 * float(n) * ls

    ent = float(ent)
    W = 2.0 * float(n) * u
    if ent != 0.0:
        c_floor = max(1e-300, 1e-10 / float(n))
        cs = np.maximum(c, c_floor)
        H = -float(np.sum(cs * np.log(cs)))
        Hmax = math.log(max(1, 2 * n - 1))
        obj += ent * (Hmax - H)
        W = W + ent * (np.log(cs) + 1.0)
        W = np.where(np.isfinite(W), W, 0.0)

    Wf = np.fft.rfft(W, N)
    gp = 2.0 * np.fft.irfft(Wf * np.conjugate(F), N).astype(np.float64)
    gp = gp[:n]
    gp = np.where(np.isfinite(gp), gp, 0.0)

    avg = float(np.dot(p, gp))
    gx = p * (gp - avg)
    gx = np.where(np.isfinite(gx), gx, 0.0)
    return float(obj), gx


def cross_objective_and_grad(x, q, Fq, alpha, n, N):
    """
    Smooth cross-correlation subproblem:

        min_b  logsumexp(beta * (q*b)) / beta

    with q fixed and b = softmax(x).
    """
    n = int(n)
    b = stable_softmax(x)
    Fb = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(np.asarray(Fq, dtype=np.complex128) * Fb, N)
    h = h[: 2 * n - 1].astype(np.float64)
    h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
    h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)

    beta = float(alpha) * float(n)
    if beta <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(beta):
        beta = 1.0

    z = beta * h
    m = float(np.max(z)) if z.size else 0.0
    ez = np.exp(np.maximum(z - m, -745.0))
    se = float(ez.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(se) or se <= 0.0:
        obj, grad = INF, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        return obj, grad

    u = ez / se
    ls = (m + math.log(se)) / beta
    obj = 2.0 * float(n) * ls

    uf = np.fft.rfft(u, N)
    gb = np.fft.irfft(uf * np.conjugate(np.asarray(Fq, dtype=np.complex128)), N)
    gb = gb[:n].astype(np.float64)
    gb = np.where(np.isfinite(gb), gb, 0.0)

    avg = float(np.dot(b, gb))
    gx = b * (gb - avg)
    gx = np.where(np.isfinite(gx), gx, 0.0)
    return float(obj), gx


def cross_update(p, b_x, n, N, deadline, rng):
    """
    Solve/optimize the convex cross subproblem for fixed q=p,
    then try mixing q with the optimized b.
    """
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    q = p.copy()
    Fq, _ = self_c(q, N)

    best_p = q.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(best_p, n, N)
    best_x = log_weights(best_p)
    improved = False

    if b_x is None or len(b_x) != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(b_x)):
        b_x = best_x.copy()

    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return best_p, best_x, b_x, False, best_score

    alpha_cross = 400.0
    for _ in range(2):
        if time.monotonic() >= float(deadline) - 20.0:
            break

        maxiter = 25 if n > 12000 else 40
        args = (q, Fq, alpha_cross, n, N)
        bx, _ = lbfgs_chunk(b_x, cross_objective_and_grad, args, maxiter, deadline)
        if bx is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(bx)):
            b_x = bx

        b = stable_softmax(b_x)

        sc_b = fft_score(b, n, N)
        if np.isfinite(sc_b) and sc_b < best_score - 1e-10:
            best_score = sc_b
            best_p = b.copy()
            best_x = log_weights(best_p)
            improved = True

        for t in (0.25, 0.40, 0.55, 0.70, 0.85):
            cand = (1.0 - t) * q + t * b
            s = float(np.sum(cand))
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
                continue
            cand = cand / s
            sc = fft_score(cand, n, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = cand.copy()
                best_x = log_weights(best_p)
                improved = True

    return best_p, best_x, b_x, improved, best_score


def exact_mirror_adam(x, n, N, deadline, rng):
    """
    Monotone mirror-descent polish using the exact maximum peak subgradient.
    Active weights are concentrated on the current largest convolution
    coefficients.
    """
    n = int(n)
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if x.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        x = log_weights(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))

    best_x = x.copy()
    best_p = stable_softmax(x)
    best_sc = fft_score(best_p, n, N)

    x_cur = best_x.copy()
    lr = 0.05
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    stall = 0
    delta = 0.20
    top_frac = 0.02

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline) - 2.0:
        p = stable_softmax(x_cur)
        F, c = self_c(p, N)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            break

        total = int(c.size)
        top_count = max(1, int(total * top_frac))
        if top_count >= total:
            idx = np.arange(total)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -top_count)[-top_count:]

        vals = c[idx]
        if M > 0.0:
            ratio = vals / M
            w = np.maximum(ratio - (1.0 - delta), 0.0)
            ws = float(np.sum(w))
            if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
                w = ratio * ratio + 1e-8
                ws = float(np.sum(w))
        else:
            w = np.ones_like(vals, dtype=np.float64)
            ws = float(w.size)

        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones_like(vals, dtype=np.float64)
            ws = float(w.size)

        u = np.zeros(total, dtype=np.float64)
        u[idx] = w / ws

        uf = np.fft.rfft(u, N)
        gp = 2.0 * np.fft.irfft(uf * np.conjugate(F), N).astype(np.float64)
        gp = gp[:n]
        gp = np.where(np.isfinite(gp), gp, 0.0)

        gx = p * (gp - float(np.dot(p, gp)))
        gx = np.where(np.isfinite(gx), gx, 0.0)

        t += 1
        b1 = 0.9
        b2 = 0.999
        m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * gx
        v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * gx * gx
        mhat = m / (1.0 - b1 ** t)
        vhat = v / (1.0 - b2 ** t)
        step = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-12)
        step = np.where(np.isfinite(step), step, 0.0)
        step = np.clip(step, -100.0, 100.0)

        maxabs = float(np.max(np.abs(step))) if step.size else 1.0
        if maxabs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(maxabs):
            maxabs = 1.0
        scale = lr / maxabs

        accepted = False
        for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.10, 0.05):
            x_try = x_cur - (scale * factor) * step
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(x_try)):
                x_try = x_cur.copy()
            p_try = stable_softmax(x_try)
            sc_try = fft_score(p_try, n, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_sc - 1e-12:
                x_cur = x_try.copy()
                best_x = x_try.copy()
                best_p = p_try.copy()
                best_sc = sc_try
                stall = 0
                accepted = True
                break

        if not accepted:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 5:
                lr = max(0.005, lr * 0.75)
                delta = min(0.8, delta + 0.05)
            elif stall == 15:
                x_cur = best_x + 0.10 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
                m.fill(0.0)
                v.fill(0.0)
                t = 0
                lr = 0.04
                stall = 0
            elif stall == 40:
                x_cur = best_x + 0.30 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
                m.fill(0.0)
                v.fill(0.0)
                t = 0
                lr = 0.06
                delta = 0.4
                stall = 0
            elif stall > 60:
                x_cur = best_x.copy()
                delta = 0.3
                stall = 0

    return best_x, best_p, best_sc


def optimize_start(p_in, time_budget, rng, tag=""):
    """Run a full optimizer on one starting sequence."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    time_budget = max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    if time_budget <= 5.0:
        p = prepare_start(p_in)
        n = p.size
        N = next_fast_len(2 * n)
        return p, fft_score(p, n, N)

    p = prepare_start(p_in)
    n = int(p.size)
    N = next_fast_len(2 * n)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_x = log_weights(p)
    best_score = fft_score(best_p, n, N)
    b_x = best_x.copy()

    hard_end = start + time_budget

    # Reserve part of the budget for the exact-peak mirror polish.
    reserve = 0.0
    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        reserve = max(20.0, 0.20 * time_budget)
    phase_end = start + max(time_budget - reserve, 0.0)

    phases = [
        (10.0, 0.00),
        (20.0, 0.00),
        (40.0, 0.00),
        (80.0, 0.00),
        (80.0, 0.05),
        (120.0, 0.10),
        (200.0, 0.20),
        (300.0, 0.30),
        (300.0, 0.00),
        (600.0, 0.10),
        (600.0, 0.30),
        (1200.0, 0.20),
        (1200.0, 0.00),
        (2500.0, 0.10),
    ]

    cross_count = 0
    cross_limit = 3 if n > 8000 else 5

    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        for phase_i, (alpha, ent) in enumerate(phases):
            if time.monotonic() >= phase_end - 20.0:
                break

            inner_no = 0
            x = best_x.copy()

            while time.monotonic() < phase_end - 30.0:
                if inner_no >= 3:
                    break

                args = (float(alpha), float(ent), int(n), int(N))
                maxiter = 25 if n > 12000 else (35 if n > 6000 else 60)
                x_new, _ = lbfgs_chunk(
                    x, self_objective_and_grad, args, maxiter, phase_end
                )
                if x_new is not None:
                    x = x_new

                p_cur = stable_softmax(x)
                sc = fft_score(p_cur, n, N)

                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_p = p_cur.copy()
                    best_x = x.copy()
                    x = best_x.copy()
                    inner_no = 0
                    if tag:
                        print(
                            f"[{tag}] alpha={alpha:.1f} ent={ent:.3f} "
                            f"score={best_score:.8f} time={phase_i+1}/{len(phases)}",
                            flush=True
                        )
                else:
                    inner_no += 1
                    sig = 0.02 * float(inner_no)
                    x = best_x + sig * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)

            if time.monotonic() < phase_end and cross_count < cross_limit:
                if (phase_i % 2 == 0) or best_score > 1.55:
                    p_new, x_new, b_x_new, improved, sc_new = cross_update(
                        best_p, b_x, n, N, phase_end, rng
                    )
                    cross_count += 1
                    b_x = b_x_new
                    if improved and np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
                        best_p = p_new.copy()
                        best_x = x_new.copy()
                        best_score = sc_new
                        if tag:
                            print(
                                f"[{tag}] cross improved score={best_score:.8f}",
                                flush=True
                            )

    # Final exact-peak polishing.
    if hard_end - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
        x_final, p_final, sc_final = exact_mirror_adam(
            best_x, n, N, hard_end - 2.0, rng
        )
        if np.isfinite(sc_final) and sc_final < best_score:
            best_score = sc_final
            best_p = p_final.copy()
            best_x = x_final.copy()

    if tag:
        print(f"[{tag}] final FFT score={best_score:.8f}", flush=True)
    return best_p, best_score


def choose_alt_length(n, factor, upper, lower):
    """Choose an alternate length rounded nicely."""
    n = int(max(64, n))
    m = int(round(float(n) * float(factor)))
    step = 128
    if m > 0:
        m = int(round(float(m) / float(step)) * step)
    m = max(int(lower), m)
    m = min(int(upper), m)
    m = min(m, MAX_SEARCH_N)
    return m


def trim_edges(p, edge_mass=1e-7):
    """Trim leading/trailing very small mass."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return p.copy()

    cum = np.cumsum(p)
    left = 0
    while left < n and cum[left] <= float(edge_mass):
        left += 1

    rev = p[::-1]
    rev_cum = np.cumsum(rev)
    right = n - 1
    while right >= 0 and rev_cum[n - 1 - right] <= float(edge_mass):
        right -= 1

    if right <= left:
        # Fall back to the largest single element region if trimming was too aggressive.
        idx = int(np.argmax(p))
        return p[idx: idx + 1].copy()
    return p[left: right + 1].copy()


def cleanup_best_limited(p, time_limit, max_variants):
    """Pick the best exact-score clean-up variant within a time limit."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    max_variants = max(1, int(max_variants))

    if n > MAX_EXACT_N:
        p = resample_seq(p, MAX_EXACT_N)
        n = int(p.size)

    variants = [p]

    if max_variants > 1:
        t1 = trim_edges(p, 1e-7)
        if t1.size > 0:
            variants.append(t1)

    if max_variants > 2:
        t2 = trim_edges(p, 1e-5)
        if t2.size > 0:
            variants.append(t2)

    if max_variants > 3:
        z = p.copy()
        mask = z < 1e-10
        if np.any(mask):
            z[mask] = 0.0
            s = float(z.sum())
            if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
                z = z / s
                variants.append(z)

    if max_variants > 4:
        z = p.copy()
        mask = z < 1e-8
        if np.any(mask):
            z[mask] = 0.0
            s = float(z.sum())
            if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
                z = z / s
                tz = trim_edges(z, 1e-7)
                if tz.size > 0:
                    variants.append(tz)

    best = p
    best_sc = INF

    for v in variants:
        if time.monotonic() - start > max(0.5, float(time_limit) * 0.75):
            break
        if v.size > MAX_EXACT_N:
            continue
        sc = exact_score(v)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = v.copy()

    if not np.isfinite(best_sc):
        best_sc = fft_score(best, best.size, next_fast_len(2 * best.size))
    return best, best_sc


def finalize_sequence(p):
    """Scale to a safe official list."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    if p.size == 0:
        p = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    n = int(p.size)
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        p = p * (target / s)
    else:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * (target / float(n))

    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    p = np.minimum(p, 1000.0)

    s2 = float(p.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 > 0.0 and s2 < 0.01:
        p = p * (0.01 / s2)
        p = np.minimum(p, 1000.0)

    return [float(x) for x in p]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Entry point invoked by the harness.

    Runs a hybrid FFT/L-BFGS/entropy/cross-correlation search and returns the
    best non-negative sequence found, scaled safely for the evaluator.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    budget = max(60.0, min(float(budget), 2395.0))

    hard_end = t0 + budget - 5.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    starts = collect_starts()
    starts = sort_starts_by_score(starts, limit=3)
    p0 = prepare_start(starts[0])

    n0 = int(p0.size)
    N0 = next_fast_len(2 * n0)
    results = []
    sc0 = fft_score(p0, n0, N0)
    results.append((p0, sc0))

    # Main search from the best known parent.
    main_time = budget * 0.58
    if main_time > 60.0:
        p_main, sc_main = optimize_start(p0, main_time, rng, tag="main")
        results.append((p_main, sc_main))

    # Optional longer alternate length.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = hard_end - now
    if rem > 300.0:
        m_up = choose_alt_length(n0, 1.6, MAX_SEARCH_N, 2048)
        if m_up != n0:
            p_up = resample_seq(p0, m_up)
            t_up = min(rem * 0.42, 800.0)
            if t_up > 60.0:
                p_up, sc_up = optimize_start(p_up, t_up, rng, tag="up")
                results.append((p_up, sc_up))

    # Optional diversified start at the main/fixed length.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = hard_end - now
    if rem > 180.0:
        m_rand = n0 if n0 <= MAX_SEARCH_N else MAX_SEARCH_N
        p_rand_a = resample_seq(p0, m_rand)
        p_rand_b = random_start(m_rand, rng)
        p_rand = normalize_nonneg(0.5 * p_rand_a + 0.5 * p_rand_b)
        t_rand = min(rem * 0.55, 450.0)
        if t_rand > 40.0:
            p_rand, sc_rand = optimize_start(p_rand, t_rand, rng, tag="rand")
            results.append((p_rand, sc_rand))

    # Final exact validation and clean-up.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = hard_end - now
    candidates = top_candidates_by_score([p for p, _ in results], k=4)
    # Make sure the raw parent is considered if it was not already in top.
    if len(candidates) < 4:
        candidates.append(p0)

    best_final = p0
    best_exact = INF
    final_start = time.monotonic()
    final_budget = max(5.0, hard_end - final_start)

    # Number of clean-up variants depends on remaining time and length.
    if final_budget > 120.0 and n0 <= 8000:
        max_variants = 5
    elif final_budget > 60.0:
        max_variants = 3
    else:
        max_variants = 2

    for i, p in enumerate(candidates):
        left = hard_end - time.monotonic()
        if left < 2.0:
            break

        # For very large sequences, do at most one exact conv unless time is ample.
        alloc = max(2.0, min(60.0, left / float(max(1, len(candidates) - i))))
        if p.size > 9000 and final_budget < 90.0:
            alloc = min(alloc, 8.0)

        v, sc = cleanup_best_limited(p, alloc, max_variants)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact:
            best_exact = sc
            best_final = v

        if time.monotonic() >= hard_end - 2.0:
            break

    if not np.isfinite(best_exact):
        # Fallback: choose by FFT score.
        best_final = candidates[0]
        bft = fft_score(best_final, best_final.size, next_fast_len(2 * best_final.size))
        best_exact = bft

    seq = finalize_sequence(best_final)
    try:
        print(
            f"propose_candidate done: len={len(seq)} exact_score={best_exact:.8f} "
            f"elapsed={time.monotonic() - t0:.1f}s",
            flush=True
        )
    except Exception:
        pass

    return seq
```
